Each run keeps up to three copies in turn, `<session>-1.sculpt` to `-3.sculpt` with a `<session>.session` file saying when they were written and by which process, in an `autosave` folder beside `settings.txt` (`%APPDATA%\Implicit\Sculpt\autosave` on Windows, `~/.local/share/Implicit/Sculpt/autosave` on Linux). Each is written beside its name, flushed to the disk and renamed over it, so killing the app, a crash or a power cut mid-write leaves the copy before it whole. A clean exit writes a last copy if anything changed (hiding the window first), keeps the newest and marks the session closed; the last three closed sessions are kept, older ones deleted. Saving the scene counts as a copy: a run that crashes after a save with nothing new is not offered.

When a run starts and finds a session whose process is no longer running and was not closed, a card at the top asks "Recover your work?" with when it was autosaved, how many objects and vertices it held and the scene file's name: Recover opens its newest copy that reads whole (Save scene then saves to the file the scene came from, if it had one, and the next autosaves continue in this run's session), Not now leaves it for the next start (Escape too), and Discard deletes it. Crashed sessions are never deleted without asking. Menu > Open the last autosave (and the command palette) opens the newest copy of any earlier session at any time, closed ones included. On tablets (Android and the iPad) the app cannot tell a crash from being closed by the system, so every session not closed is offered.

Disk use is three copies of the scene a run (about 360 MB each at 10M) and the last three closed runs; a 10M scene's copy takes about 5 s to read and 2.6 s to write on the VM, at most once a minute and only after a change. Strokes while a copy is written cost the same (`build.sh release` then `build/sculpt --vertices=10000000 --bench-stroke=240 --no-vsync`, with and without `--autosave=1 --bench-after-autosave`, three runs each: brush p50 0.64 to 0.73 ms either way; frame p50 16.0 to 17.5 ms without, 17.5 to 18.0 ms with, where the 4-core VM's software GPU uses every core the writer also uses).

Flags: `--autosave=SECONDS` autosave that often (scripted runs only autosave with it or `--autosave-folder`), `--autosave-folder=DIR` keep the copies in DIR and offer the crashed ones found there, `--autosave-rest=SECONDS` the pause before a copy starts (default 1.5), `--check-autosave` hash what each copy read before and after and count a difference as `autosave_torn`, `--crash-after-autosave` exit with code 3 right after the first copy, skipping the clean exit (the crash test), `--bench-after-autosave` start `--bench-stroke` once a copy of the starting scene is being written. The exit stats give `autosaves`, `autosave_aborts`, `autosave_torn`, `autosave_wait_ms`, `autosave_sync_ms` (bringing the levels up to date on the frame loop before a copy), `autosave_serialize_ms`, `autosave_write_ms`, `autosave_mb`, `recovered` and `recover_offers`. `scripts/touch_check.py 'autosave_*'` runs the crash, recover, discard, Not now, stroke-stops-it and Off scenarios.
## Interface

The interface is touch-first, designed in Figma ("Sculpt UI"), and drawn by the app's own kit (`src/ui`). One layout serves every OS and screen, scaled by the screen's pixel density and the system's display scaling (Android's screen density, Windows' Scale setting), laid out inside the window's safe area, and every control's hit area is at least 48 points (the App Store's 44 pt and Google Play's 48 dp both met) while what is drawn inside it stays compact.

**Layout: Lens.** The app has one layout, Lens, built after the Sculpt Workspace Directions mockups (option C); the ZBrush-iPad layout it grew from, Classic, is gone, and a settings file's `chrome`, `panes_under_panels`, `dock`, `dock_count`, `dock_pack` and `right_rail` lines from then are passed over. Its **chip bar** (`src/app/kit_top_lens.odin`, the overhaul's round 3) floats across the top, 8 points in from the sides, as one rounded bar whose sections hairlines split: the menu (`src/app/lens_menu.odin`, below), the file block (the scene's file name over "Saved 2 min ago", "Unsaved changes" or "Not saved", which counts from the last save or from when the file opened was written), Undo and Redo (their tooltips name the step; a long press or a right-click on either opens History) at its left; the spaces as tabs in the middle, Sculpt, Model and Render, each tinted its own colour when lit (a tap on another switches to it, as Ctrl+1 to 3 do; the lit one carries the amber dot while its layout has changes not saved; a tap on it or on the chevron after them opens its layouts; a window too narrow for the tabs shows the layout's chip; see Spaces and workspaces); the **object chip** (`src/app/object_chip.odin`): the selected object's type icon, its name, its type (Sculpt mesh, Voxel clay or Cage) and up to three badges (its level as L2, HD, Dynamic, its layers; a cage's Smooth steps), whose tap opens the object's menu under it and whose long press (or right-click) opens the Scene palette (with nothing selected it counts the scene's objects and a tap opens the Scene palette); and at its right the Search field ("Search commands, brushes, panels", with its key, Ctrl+K: a tap opens the command palette under it; a narrow window shows only its icon), History and Settings. As the window narrows the object chip drops its badges, then its type, before Search shrinks to its icon; then the chip shrinks to its icon, and last the tabs give way to the layout's chip. Exit stats: `object_chip=` (how much of it showed, then its type and badges, as `Full,Sculpt_mesh,L2`) and `view_chips=`. Edit pies is in Settings and Search, Help in the menu. **The Lens menu** is one list: Home; New ›; Open...; Open recent ›; Save, Save as..., Save a copy... and Earlier saves...; Import › and Export... (the Export sheet, see Files); Workspaces ›; Customise...; Settings; Help and shortcuts; More ›. A row with a chevron opens its submenu beside the list (mirrored for left-handed) on a tap, or when a mouse rests on it for a quarter of a second; a press outside closes both. New holds every new scene start (the starters, Cube, Plane, Voxel block and Empty scene) and each content pack's meshes (a first tap on a scene with work asks "Discard?"); Open recent the last eight scenes with when each was saved ("Open now" for the one open, "Not found" for one gone, which a tap takes off Recent), then Open the last autosave, and a scene with work to lose asks "Discard changes and open?" first; Import: Import mesh, Get from Blender (desktop) and Import workspace; Workspaces: each workspace, ticked when current, Export workspace and Workspace options...; More: Stats, Macros, Library, Reduce motion and Send to Blender (desktop). **Lens's search** opens 520 points wide, centred under the top bar (`src/app/lens_search.odin`). While something is typed its results stand in three groups, COMMANDS, BRUSHES and PANELS AND SETTINGS, each in the order it ranked. Each row says where it lives under its name: "Shelf · Sculpt" or "Brush picker" for a brush, "Object palette · Mask" for a control in the tray, "View rail", "Brush pie", "Menu" or "Menu · Export...", "Top bar", "Settings", or "Panel · Layers card" for a section; nothing for what only keys or gestures run. The picked row has its shortcut and **Put on a bar**, which puts a brush at the end of the shelf's page shown and anything else at the end of the view rail (one there already stays, with a note). The footer reads "Enter run · Tab show where it lives · Ctrl+Enter put on a bar", with Star, Put in a pie and Assign key for the pick; Tab opens the tray on the control's section or names where it lives in a note. **Focus**: Focus mode (or a layout that hides the view rail) turns the top bar into three islands over the view: Menu, Undo and Redo at the off-hand corner; the workspace chip (a tap opens the switcher's menu) and an X symmetry chip, lit while it is on, in the middle; Search, View layout, Passes and History at the pen side. The view's chips and the rail stand aside; the off-hand island (Size and Strength) and the dial stay; the shelf shows seven tiles and All, without its page names. The exit stats add `scene_filter` (1 while a filter is typed), `scene_rows`, `object_locks`, `layer_rows`, `layer_picked`, `layer_recording` (−1 for none), `tools_tab`, `mask_hidden`, `selected_level` (from 0), `lens_focus`, `menu_sub` (the submenu open), `menu_subs`, `menu_recent_opens`, `palette_bar_puts` and `palette_groups` (the groups the results stood in, as `Commands,Brushes`). The view fills the window and everything floats over it (`src/app/lens_view.odin`): the chip bar floats over the picture with the view showing round it, the view grid always runs under the panels, and the model is drawn in the middle of the room the bars and panels leave rather than of the window: the camera's target is drawn off centre there (the camera's shift), so it still turns about the model. Frame all, Focus selected and Reset camera put it there again and back the camera off to fit that room's shorter side; the start does it once. A panel a stroke passes under fades to a quarter while the stroke lasts and comes back when the pen lifts; the stroke goes on under it. **Clean view** (Tab; a four-finger tap, its default gesture; the command Clean view; Escape, Tab, the tap or the hint at the top bring the interface back) hides every control, keeping only the dial unless Settings > Interface > Keep the dial in clean view is off. Round the view stands the chrome (`src/app/lens_chrome.odin`), mirrored whole in the left-handed layout:
- **The off-hand island** stands on the left edge above the dial (a workspace's layout hides it as its `brush_bar` zone). It is a bar like the others (`offhand_island` in the settings file, up to 8 items, yours rather than a workspace's, as the shelf's pages are): at first Size and Strength as tall sliders that a thumb drags without looking, and Customise bars puts any command or brush value on it, as Classic's toolbars held them. The pen held over Size or Strength without touching and turned about its own length (where the platform reports the pen's roll: the Apple Pencil Pro on iPadOS 17.5, Windows Ink and some Android pens) sets it too: a quarter turn clockwise (to the right, seen from the pen's top) doubles the size, half a turn takes the strength from 0 to 1, and anticlockwise the other way. The first 10 degrees the pen turns on a control set nothing, since a hand turns the pen a little as it carries it over a slider on the way elsewhere; past them every turn counts, back as well as on, until the pen leaves the control. A pen that comes back turned, or whose roll jumps further than a hand turns between two reports (45 degrees), starts its turn afresh rather than jumping the value. On the iPad, SDL passes UIKit's roll on with the opposite sign to every other platform's, which the app turns round (`pencil_roll_uikit`), so that the Pencil Pro turned to the right raises a value there too (from a report of the Pencil Pro turning the wrong way; the turned sign is not yet tried on an iPad). The same roll moves every other slider and dragged number the pen hovers on, in the panels and Settings alike: half a turn clockwise sweeps a slider from one end to the other, and a dragged number moves as a point of drag a degree (`src/ui/widgets.odin`, `ROLL_SWEEP`). The brushes are on the shelf and in the brush picker, the stroke, alpha and material from the tray, and Add and Subtract are the dial's Invert.
- **Brush values on any bar** (`src/app/brush_values.odin`): Size, Strength, Focal shift, Spacing, Lazy and Pressure, each with its own icon (Lucide's radius, gauge, focus, ellipsis, spline and signature), go on the island, the view rail, the shelf's tools and pages, and the brush pie; Search's Put on a bar puts one on the island, and Search, a key or the pie opens the value's popover at the pointer. Each item has a look of its own: **Slider** (the island's tall slider; the island only, as the rail scrolls and the shelf swipes), **Chip** (the icon over the number with a hairline of how far it is set: a tap opens the popover, a drag across the bar scrubs it, sideways on the island and the rail, up and down on the shelf so a sideways swipe still turns the page) or **Icon** (the icon alone: a tap opens the popover). The popover has the exact value, a wide slider and steps to tap (Size 12 to 192 px, Strength in quarters, Focal shift -50, 0 and 50, Spacing 5 to 50 %, Lazy off to 60 px); Pressure's has its two switches, pressure on the size and on the strength, and is only tapped. A long press (or a right-click) on any value opens its menu: Show as Slider, Chip or Icon, Brush settings (the value's section: the Pen section for Size, Strength and Pressure, Falloff for Focal shift, Stroke for Spacing and Lazy), Move, Add another value and Remove from the bar. Spacing is dimmed unless the stroke is Dots. A window too short for every slider on the island shows the last ones as chips. Each look is kept in the settings file as a `bar_look <zone> <slot> <look>` line after its slot, which an older build passes over.
- **The view chips** sit over the view's top-left corner:
  - the camera (its dot, Persp or Ortho and its lens in mm; a tap opens the Camera menu);
  - the pass (a tap opens the list, a hold peeks);
  - Clean view.
  They are the main view's, so they stand aside while another pane is maximised over it, whose head has its own.
- **The view rail** stands down the right edge: the tray's button, then a customisable bar (`view_rail` in the settings file and workspace layouts) whose eight first buttons open beside it:
  - View layout (the view grid's presets, the render preview pane, a view window and the guides; the command View layout);
  - Passes (the command Passes);
  - Camera;
  - Frame (Focus selected);
  - Symmetry;
  - Wireframe;
  - Lights (the Lights section);
  - Mini view.
  
  Customise bars changes it as it does the others, and a section put on any bar opens beside its button.
- **The navigation head** stands left of the rail or the tray at the top: a small head that turns with the camera (its face is the front, +Z), with the X, Y and Z axes in red, green and blue at its foot. A tap on one of its sides looks at the scene from that side (front, back, left, right, top or bottom, as the keypad's views do), and a drag on it turns the view as dragging empty space does.
- **The mini view** is Lens's thumbnail: the second camera's picture alone, 168 by 200 points, at the bottom of the right side under the panels. On a tablet-shaped window, no wider than 1.5 times its height, it keeps a fifth of the height clear for the pen hand's palm. A chip on the picture names its camera and pass; a tap on the chip shows the camera and pass choices with Flip, Link and Close under the picture. A tap on the picture swaps it with the main view: the main view takes its camera, and it keeps the main view's as a free camera, so a second tap swaps back. A linked mini view has nothing to swap. A drag turns it.

- **The shelf** stands at the bottom (left of the middle on a tablet-shaped window, clear of the palm, where it shows at most its first eight tiles): a strip of the page's tiles with All at their end, then after a divider the tools, and under it the names of the space's pages: Sculpt, Paint, Move and Curve in Sculpt (and Render, whose brush picks go back to Sculpt), Model alone in Model; each space comes back on the page it was left on.
  - The tools are the gizmo (the Move tool; a long press shows its settings), Remesh, Dynamic topology (on or off), Retopologize, Subdivide (adds a level) and Live boolean (on or off), the same on every page but Model, whose cage has tools of its own on the page. A tap runs each, Retopologize's opens its section, and a long press on any opens its section of the Object palette, except Remesh's, which opens its quick menu over it (after Nomad's voxel popup): Density, with Half, Same and Double of the object's own vertex count a tap away and the remesh's cells shown on the object while it is held (as in Object > Shape > Rebuild), one big Remesh that rebuilds the object at that density and leaves the menu open to try again, and More options for the Rebuild section itself (fewer polygons, quads). Arrange changes them as it does the pages, and Reset zone on the shelf puts them back too; they belong to the workspace's layout.
  - The shelf keeps a dial's reach clear at each side. Where the window is too narrow for everything, the brushes give way to six first, then the tools to three, then the brushes to three; what does not fit is still in All and in Search.
  - A finger or the pen swiped sideways along the shelf turns its page, as a home screen's pages turn: the page's brushes follow the finger with the next page coming in behind them, a swipe past about a fifth of the row turns it (to the left for the next page, Sculpt to Paint to Move to Curve to Model), and past Sculpt or Model the row only gives a little. The mouse wheel over the shelf turns a page a notch, and a tap on a page's name goes to it. The exit stats add `shelf_swipes` (pages a swipe or the wheel turned), `shelf_tools` and `shelf_shown` (the tools and brushes laid out).
  - Sculpt starts with Clay, Draw, Smooth, Inflate, Pinch, Crease, Grab, Flatten, Mask and Layer; Paint with Paint, Mask, Smooth, Colors, Swap colours and the paint channel; Move with Grab, Snake hook, Cloth, the gizmo and Smooth; Curve with the Curve and Insert tools and Smooth; Model with Extrude, Inset, Move, Delete, Insert loop, Remove loop, Bridge, Close hole and Weld (each takes the Model tool), Add cage for a cube and a cylinder, and Apply to sculpt (Model mode).
  - A tap on a tile picks its brush. A long press picks it and shows its quick settings above the tile, so the hand never covers them: its name and favourite heart, Radius, Strength, the stroke (Dots, Rectangle, Spray, Roll, Drag dot), the alpha (Round and the first five of the library) and the lazy mouse, then Pin as panel (the Stroke section as a panel) and All settings (the brush's section in the tray). A tap elsewhere closes them.
  - Each page is a bar of its own (`shelf_sculpt`, `shelf_paint`, `shelf_move`, `shelf_curve` in the settings file, up to 12 tiles), changed by Customise bars as the others are. They hold your brushes, so a workspace's layout leaves them out.
- **The brush picker** opens from All, or B (the command Brush picker), as a large sheet over the shelf, which stays in sight under it:
  - the categories down its left in the app's order (Recent, Favorites, All, then by what a brush does, your presets and each installed pack), with how many each holds;
  - a search field, the shelf's page names (which narrow All to what that page is for, and turn the shelf too), Import (a content pack) and Close;
  - every brush, tool and preset as a card showing its stroke laid on clay (an arc for Draw, a wide one for Inflate, a groove for Crease and Pinch, a hook for Grab and Snake hook, ripples for Cloth, a flat band for Flatten, Layer and the smooths), with a heart on a favourite.
  
  A tap on a card picks it and closes the picker; a long press shows its settings. A card dragged out of the grid follows the finger, and let go over a shelf tile it takes that tile's place on the page shown (a brush or a tool; a preset stays in the picker).

- **The panel stack** is the palette tray: the tray's button at the top of the rail shows or hides it, and it docks, floats and opens wherever the tray does. It stands down the pen side under the top bar, 312 points wide, as cards:
  - **Brush** (the tool's name and settings with another tool): the brush's card (its stroke, name, family, "edited" once its preset has changed, and the favourite heart), Radius and Strength, then the Brush palette's sections as groups that fold, each saying how it is set while folded ("Dots · lazy 30 px", "pressure → size, strength"). Stroke, Falloff and Alpha start open, the rest folded.
  - **Symmetry**, with X, Y and Z as chips in its header, then the axes, Local and the radial copies.
  - **Layers** (`src/app/lens_layers.odin`; its header names the object): the selected object's layers newest on top, each a row with its eye, its name, its strength and a dot, red while strokes go into it. A tap on the name picks the layer, whose row grows to hold its Strength slider (−1 to 2, ±5 with Wide strength; a drag is one undo step); a tap on the dot records into that layer or stops. Under them **Base**, the surface the layers sit on ("locked" while a layer records, as strokes then go only into it), **New layer** (which records into the new one), and More options: the picked layer's name, Delete and Bake, Duplicate and Invert, Merge down and Bake all, Move up and Move down, and Wide strength.
  - **Scene** (`src/app/lens_scene.odin`; its header says how many objects): the objects as rows with each one's picture, name and triangles, its eye and its **lock**, and the folders with their counts; past ten objects a **Filter objects** field keeps the rows whose names hold what is typed (any case), with the folders they are in. A tap selects (Ctrl, Shift or Select several adds), a drag reorders a row or puts it in a folder, the eyes sweep down the rows, and a long press or right-click opens the object's menu. A locked object still draws but takes no press in the view: strokes, taps, the gizmo's pick and the curve tool pass over it to what is behind, and the selection moves to the next object, as for a ghost; Ctrl and a tap on a lock locks every other object instead (ZBrush's Freeze others), and a tap on a locked row unlocks and selects it. Like an eye, a lock is not an undo step. Then **SELECTION SETS**, the selected object's stored masks with how much each masks: a tap puts that set back as the mask, a long press opens the Sets section on it (Add, Subtract, Store over, Rename, Delete), and Store mask as set adds one.
  - **Tools** (`src/app/lens_tools.odin`; its header names the tab), in four tabs. **Mask**: Invert, Clear, Blur, Sharpen, Grow, Shrink, By cavity, By angle (Mask by Facing away) and Extract as a grid of big buttons with their keys under them, then Steps (how far Blur, Grow and their kin reach) and **Show the mask** (off, the view draws masked parts as the rest, as ZBrush's View mask; the mask stays, and it is not an undo step). **Visibility**: Show all, Invert visible, Hide masked, Delete hidden, Grow and Shrink, then the objects' stored visibilities V1 to V8. **Geometry**: REMESH (Half, Same or Double the object's vertices, Density, which shows the cells while held, and Remesh), SUBDIVISION (each level as a numbered chip, a tap going to it, and Divide), Dynamic topology, and MODIFY TOPOLOGY's six repairs as a grid. **Deform**: the Deformation section. Each button is the command of its name, so its key, gesture and whether it can run come from the registry; the full sections stay under Palettes. A stack line saved before the card puts it after Scene.
  - **Palettes**, which holds the whole tray (every palette and section) until later rounds give those sections panels of their own.
  - The look's cards (`src/app/lens_studio.odin`), which a fresh layout leaves out and Studio shows. **Lights**: each light a row with its colour, its name and "5200 K · 3.0" (its temperature, when it has one, and its intensity); a tap picks it, and the picked row grows to hold Intensity and Size (the angle of a sun, the radius of a lamp), a drag one undo step. Then Add light (a point light, picked) and Lights follow the camera (off, every light stays with the scene, as on a turntable). **Material**: nine lit presets as spheres, the one in use ringed (in the last place when it is not one of the nine), a tap putting it on the selected objects, a "+" for the rest in the Material section, and Roughness, which makes the material Custom. **Environment**: the Environment section as it is. The Lights and Material cards' More holds All light settings and All material settings, which open the full section under Palettes.
  
  Each card's header has a grip (drag it up or down the stack to move the card, or well clear of it to float the card there), the chevron and title (a tap folds or unfolds it; folded, it says what it holds), Float (Dock on a floating card, whose dots also drag it and, held, dock it) and More (Move up, Move down, Float, Leave out of the stack, Fold the others, Where the stack goes). Floating cards, floating panels and windows stack in one order: the one floated or touched last is in front of all the others (exit stat `float_front`), and the dial is drawn over all of them and over every bar, so a finger on it is always the dial's; only what opens for a moment (a menu, a popover, a sheet) comes over it while open. Opening a section (the object chip, Search's Show where, a bar's section, the rail's Lights) opens its card, putting it back in the stack when it was left out, and folds Brush and Palettes for another. The order, the open cards and the ones left out are part of a workspace's layout (`stack Brush+ Symmetry Layers Scene Tools Palettes -Lights -Material -Environment`, a `-` before a card left out, written only once it differs from a fresh layout's); the groups' folds last for the session.
- **Resizing the stack** (screen resize2): sizes snap to an 8 point grid, and a double tap on a handle puts it back.
  - The stack's inner edge (a short bar beside the cards; on the right of the stack when left-handed) drags the stack from 260 to 440 points wide. A pill shows the width and a dashed line where it started. A double tap gives back 312.
  - An open card's bottom grip sets how much of it shows: the card scrolls inside when its content is taller. Dragged up past its header, the grip folds the card. A double tap fits it to its content again.
  - A floating card resizes from any of its eight handles (corners and edge middles, 36-point targets that reach only 8 points into the card). The size shows under it while it changes; a drag across keeps the height fitting the content.
  - The width and the docked cards' heights are part of a workspace's layout (`stack_size 340 Brush=320`), so Sculpt and Studio can keep different ones. A floating card's size lasts for the session.
  - Settings > Interface > Size scales all of the interface at once.
- **Split views** (screen split2), the View grid (below):
  - Each view pane's head is a row of chips: its camera (the colour dot, then "Persp linked" or "Ortho linked" while it follows the main camera, the axis with "ortho", "Mirrored linked", or "Own camera free") and what it shows, each opening its list, then Link, Maximise and Open in a window.
  - A divider has a grip in its middle and a 24 point hold for a finger. A double tap evens the split out. Dragged to the far edge, it dims the panes beyond with "Let go to close", and letting go there closes them (one plus two and four step down to two panes, two panes to Single); toward the main view it says "The main view stays", since the main view never closes.
  - Camera > View layout draws the six presets as small pictures of their panes, then **New panes start as**: Front + Side (the default, each preset's own partners), Same camera (linked to the main camera) or Copy view (an independent camera where the main view is now); `new_panes` in the settings file. Then the proportion guides, the render preview pane and a view window.
  - While panes show beside the main one, the stack becomes a strip of tabs, one a card, so the panes keep their room; a tap opens that card over the view (Float puts it on its own, Esc closes it).
  - The shelf stands under the main pane, with as many tiles as fit across it.
  - Exit stats: `grid_closes= grid_evens= new_panes= pane_menu= stack_strip= stack_tab= stack_tabs=`.
- **Pane gestures** (screens split3 and areaGest), after Blender's areas and its area docking. The view grid is a tree of splits of up to four panes, so a layout need not be a preset:
  - **Split**: every pane corner has a small bracket (always shown on touch and pen, near the pointer with a mouse; a 44 point target inside the corner the chrome leaves free). Drag it inward: sideways cuts the pane into left and right, up or down into top and bottom. The line, the share ("Split · 43 / 57") and the new part ("New pane · copies Front") follow the finger; Ctrl snaps to ¼, ⅓, ½, ⅔ and ¾. The new pane is the part on the corner's side and copies the pane it was cut from (from the main view: an independent camera where it looks now, with its pass). A fifth pane is refused with a shake and "Up to four panes". The brackets are off while you sculpt and for a moment after, so a stroke begun near a corner stays a stroke.
  - **Join**: carry the same drag out of the pane into a neighbour that shares a whole edge with it, and let go: the pane you dragged from grows over it. The main view never closes ("The main view stays"), and panes that do not line up say so. Back to the corner, Esc or a second finger cancels.
  - **Swap and dock**: drag a view pane by its camera chip (a tap still lists the cameras). Over another pane five targets show: its middle third swaps the two (no view is lost; the main view can move this way), the four sides between the diagonals dock the pane there, taking half of that pane.
  - **Dividers**: dragging one keeps the dividers inside it where they are (three across moves only the one held); Ctrl snaps, and Shift moves every divider lined up with it. A right click or a long press opens Split here (the pane on the side pressed, cut across through the point), Join (keeping either side, when both are single panes), Swap sides and Even out.
  - **Undo**: each gesture, a divider drag and a View layout preset are layout steps: Ctrl+Z takes them back and Ctrl+Shift+Z does them again, in order with the scene's steps (sixteen are kept; a workspace switch forgets them).
  - Panes keep their numbers and colours through the gestures; when one closes, the last takes its number. The main view's chips stand at the top of the main pane wherever it now is.
  - A layout keeps the tree as `panes v600 0 h500 1 2` (a split's way and share in thousandths, or a pane), with `pane` lines as before; an older layout's `grid <preset> <x> <y>` still reads and is written as its tree once.
  - Exit stats: `grid_tree= grid_splits= grid_joins= grid_swaps= grid_docks= grid_undos= grid_redos= grid_refused= grid_cancels= grid_undo_steps=`; `grid=` names the preset the tree is, or Custom.

The Shading menu (wireframe, flat shading, polygroups, transparency; Render, occlusion while working, Full render and the looks; the texture views; and Lights and look settings, the Studio section) and the Camera menu (orthographic, the field of view, focus, frame all, reset camera, previous and next view, the thumbnail, the proportion guides, the view grid and Camera settings) open beside their bar buttons (the rail's Camera, or Shading put on a bar), and both are also in the palette and the pies' choices. A long press on Wireframe opens what the polyframe shows (Lines, Fill or both), on Symmetry the axes, Local and the radial copies, and on Render and Floor grid (when put on a bar) their sections. A button with a small triangle in its corner has such a long press. **Customising the bars.** The view rail's buttons, the shelf's tools and each of the shelf's pages hold whatever you choose (`src/app/bars.odin`), as Nomad lets any tool be a shortcut. Arrange (Menu > Customise..., Settings > Interface > Bars, or the palette's "arrange" or "customise"; `src/app/lens_arrange.odin`, after round 2's custom2 screen) rings every button they hold and a tap then runs nothing: it opens the button's popover, with Move earlier and Move later, Remove, Add a button after it, and Another command..., which chooses one in Search (any command Search runs, Search itself too). A + ends each bar to add one at its end. While it is on, the top bar's workspace pill gives way to "Arranging Sculpt" (with the amber dot while the layout has changed), Library, Reset zone, Reset workspace (Revert), Save as new and Done (or Escape), Done alone on a window too narrow for them all. Each zone shows its outline and a name tag, Off-hand, Dial, "Shelf · page 1 of 4" (counted among the space's pages), Panels and "Rail · 8"; a tap on a tag picks that zone (the tag fills) and Reset zone puts it back as it comes: the dial's sectors, size and turn; the shelf's page shown and its tools; the stack of cards; the rail's buttons and place; the island's place (its items are yours, so they stay). **Moving the island and the rail** (`src/app/bar_place.odin`): their tags are grips too. A drag on one past the slop, by finger, pen or mouse, carries the bar's outline with it, the Library stepping aside, and the edges it may stand on light as strips down the window's sides, the one near the pointer brighter. Let go within 72 points of the left or right edge: the bar stands on it at that height, snapped to the edge's middle near it (Forger's toolbox) and to where it comes at its own spot; a bar dropped on the edge the other holds swaps places with it. Let go elsewhere: it floats there. An edge bar keeps under the view chips on their side and under the top bar on the other, above the dial on the dial's side and above the palm's band on the pen side, and its popovers open away from its edge (a floating bar's toward the window's middle); the panels' columns, the cards and the navigation head keep clear of whatever bar stands on their side. The place is the layout's, as it is the screen's (the dial's place, the hand's, is a setting), so each space and workspace keeps its own, Reset workspace and export keep it, and left-handed mirrors it with every side: a `bar_place offhand|rail Left|Right|Float <along> <x> <y>` line, only for a bar not where it comes (an older build passes over it, and a line it cannot take leaves the bar where it comes). Exit stats: `bar_places=` (as `offhand:right:0.25,rail:float:640,120`, or `default`) and `bar_carries=`. The Library is a sheet over the view with two tabs: Panels (a Show chip for each bar, Off-hand island, View rail, Shelf, Wheel and Next steps, which hides or shows it in this workspace, then every panel with Hidden, Left, Right or Float to put it there) and Dial: the current space's Top, Right, Bottom and Left ("MODEL DIAL SECTORS" in Model), each Smooth, Invert, Mask, Hide (Snap and Alt for Smooth and Invert in Model), Brush pie or Choose a command; Reset zone puts every space's dial back... (Search, to put any command a bar may hold on it), then the dial's Size, what its sectors show and Keep the dial in clean view. A command's sector shows its icon and name, lights while the command is on, and runs on a tap, as the pie's sector does: only a modifier's sector holds a finger. With no sector of Sculpt's dial holding Smooth or Mask the Library says so, since a tablet has nothing else that gives them. The sectors are settings (`dial_sector N modifier NAME` or `dial_sector N command NAME`, written only when they differ from Smooth, Brush pie, Invert and Mask), not part of a workspace, since they belong to the hand. Exit stats: `dial_sectors= arrange_zone= arrange_resets= library= library_tab=`. While arranging, both side columns show their outlines, an empty one saying "Drop a panel here", and a hidden shelf, wheel or next-steps bar shows as an outline where it sits, with a button to show it again. The rail holds up to 12 buttons (it scrolls when the window is too short), the tools up to 8 and each shelf page up to 12. A button keeps what it does wherever it sits: Remesh's long press is its quick menu, Shading and Camera open their menus, Symmetry, Wireframe, Render, Floor grid, the gizmo and the mesh tools keep their long presses, and Orthographic's opens the Camera menu. Remembered as `bar_count <zone> N` and `bar <zone> <slot> <command>` lines (zones `view_rail`, `bottom_tools`, `shelf_sculpt`, `shelf_paint`, `shelf_move`, `shelf_curve`, `shelf_model` and `brush_pie`), written only for a bar that differs from a fresh install's; an older build skips them and keeps its defaults.

The modifier wheel, Smooth, Mask, Invert and the brush pie, sits at the bottom left (see Touch and pen below). Over it, the action bar offers what usually comes next for the selected object, and shows only while something applies: with the Move tool, the gizmo's Move, Turn and Scale and the Snap and Pivot latches; with a mask, Invert, Clear, Blur, Grow (each by the Mask section's Steps), By cavity and Extract (ringed in the accent colour); with hidden parts, Show all, Invert and Delete hidden, the rest being on the Tools card; with an HD window open, Leave HD; right after a remesh, decimate, mirror and weld, retopology, extract, Smooth all, a deformer or a Model mode drag, that operation's name and Adjust... (see Adjust last operation below; a stroke's amount is adjusted without the bar). Each button runs the same command as its menu row and shortcut, with the same tooltip. Where the groups do not fit beside the panels (a narrow window, Settings open), each folds to one button that opens its actions above it, and Escape or Back closes that; where not even that fits, the bar waits. It dims while a stroke or a turn of the view is under way, gives way to the History panel, which sits in the same place, and notes rise above it. Settings > Interface turns it off. Under the top bar, clear of the island, the rail and the stack (lower down where it would meet the object chip), the first run shows three tips one at a time, each going once it has been done: sculpt on the model, turn the view, move and zoom it (worded for the mouse, a finger or the pen, as Settings > Touch has them; a two-finger tap is not a move). A later one done first counts too, the card moves on once the gesture is over, it shows with the Sculpt tool only and hides under menus, the palette, Settings and Help, and Skip tips ends them. Help > Gestures and the palette ("Show the first-run tips") start them again; how far they got is remembered.

The palette tray is the panel stack (above), whose Palettes card holds its three palettes, as ZBrush for iPad's palettes do, each a list of sections shown one at a time: the tool in use (Brush, or Move, Curve or Insert), Object and Scene. A field at the card's top picks the palette and section ("Object: Mask"); each palette opens on the section it showed last. Brush: the held brush and what it does over one of Preset (below), Stroke (size, strength, the stroke mode with Spray's spread or the Cloth brush's mode; under More the lazy mouse, Smoothing, Pressure smoothing, Catch up on lift, Line, Join strokes and Last stroke), Colour (with the Paint brush), History brush (what it paints back to, with Earlier, Later and Use the oldest, while it is held), Falloff (a curve to drag, and five presets), Samples (Stabilize orientation, Sample radius and Accumulate), Alpha (thumbnails to tap, Load image, mode, angle), Pen (the pressure switches, the pressure curve and its presets, tilt), Options (front faces only, Lock plane and Connected only, with All depth for Grab and Smooth only for Smooth) and the Stroke recorder; Curve and Insert show their settings and Move what it does, the gizmo's Move, Turn and Scale, Snap, and Set pivot and Reset pivot. Object: the selected object's name and size over one of its sections, listed by what is asked of it: its shape (Transform: position and scale, values to drag sideways, and the turns and Reset transform; Subdivision levels, HD detail, Dynamic topology, Rebuild, Modify topology), which part is worked on (Mask, Selection sets, Polygroups, Hide, clip and slice), its surface (Layers, Polypaint, Morph target, Deformation's sliders, Deformers) and what is made from it (UVs and baking, Textures, Project detail, Booleans, Extract). Scene: Objects (the objects, with a menu on a right-click or a long press: focus, duplicate, delete), Add object, Look, Material, Lights, Environment, Render, Lens, Camera, Turntable, 3D print, Floor grid and Background. Each palette keeps the section it shows until another is picked. Search finds every section by name ("Object: Polypaint", or just "polypaint") and opens the tray on it (the Show section command, which scripts run as `show_section paint`), and for a command whose button lives in a section, a long press (or right-click) on its row, or Shift+Enter, shows where: the tray opens on that section with its More options open and a ring pulses round the button for a moment; the help line under the list names the section. Each section shows at most five controls at first: where a section has more, the rest wait under More options at its end (a tap opens them and the row then reads Fewer options). They are: the Stroke recorder's Recorded brushes, Instant, Save, Load and Clear; Transform's turns and Reset transform; Mask's Blur, Sharpen, Grow, Shrink, Mask cavities, Mask hidden, Mask region, Mask by and Mask adjust (Steps stays out, since the action bar and the Mask taps use it); Polygroups' Angle, Groups by creases, normals, pieces and UVs, Merge similar and Clear groups; Hide, clip and slice's Grow, Shrink, Grow all, Outer ring, Grow to groups and Show masked; Retopology's bake size and reach; Project detail's Polypaint too and As placed; Material's Metallic and Roughness; Lights' colour, kind, cone, Moves with, Delete, All fixed to the scene, Turn all, Height all, Use as starting lights and Reset lights; Environment's Background and Blur; Look's Reach; Deformation's Repeat on the other objects and Smooth all with its options; Modify topology's Leave the largest hole, Delete half, Weld distance, Weld points, Check mesh and Fix mesh; Camera's distance; Floor grid's Axes in colour, Fit cells to the scene and Cell size. Settings > Interface > Show every option shows everything without the rows. The tray's section scrolls with the wheel, with a mouse drag that starts where no control takes it, and with a finger or the pen dragged up or down from anywhere on it, controls included (see Touch and pen below). Layer names are text fields: a tap gives one the keys (and, on a tablet, the on-screen keyboard), and Enter, Escape or a tap elsewhere ends it. The History button opens the History panel over the shelf (see History timeline and time-lapse), and F11 or Menu > More > Stats the Stats panel at the top left, under the object chip (see Statistics below). Menu > Settings opens Settings (see Settings below), Menu > Help or F1 the Help window in the tray's place (see Help below), Menu > More > Macros the Macros panel (see Macros) and Menu > More > Library the Library panel (see Content packs): one of them at a time, and the rail's tray button puts the tray back in their place. The tray, Stats, History, Settings, Help, Macros and Library panels are windows, as on a desktop: each has a title bar with its name, Minimise and Close. Drag the title bar (a finger or the pen once past the slop) to move the panel anywhere; it keeps to the window edges nearest it, so one moved to the right stays on the right when the window or the tablet changes size, and its title bar always stays on screen. Minimise folds it to its title bar where it is (a tap on the title, or Restore, opens it again); Close hides it until it is opened again the usual way (the rail's tray button, the History button, F11 or Menu > More > Stats, Menu > Settings, Menu > Help or F1, Menu > More > Macros, Menu > More > Library). A press on a panel brings it in front of the others. Where each panel is and whether it is folded are remembered; Settings > Interface > Reset panel positions puts them all back. The brush browser has a Close button too; menus close on a press outside them. A list (a field with a chevron) too long for the room above or below it is cut to fit the window and scrolls, by the wheel or a drag, opening at the option chosen. Hovering a control for about half a second shows its name and shortcut, and so does holding a finger or the pen still on it for the Hold delay (a long press, 0.25 s by default), for as long as it is held: letting go then does not press it. Every button, switch, tab and menu row that runs a command shows its shortcut and, where there is one, its touch gesture as the Settings have it ("Ctrl+Z · Two-finger tap, hold to repeat", "F · Double tap empty space", "Mask on: tap the mask"; menu rows show the gesture on hover), and the command palette's help line says it too; Escape closes any open menu, and a press outside one closes it without sculpting. File results and errors show as notes at the bottom that fade away, or go at a click. So do the operations that rebuild, add or remove objects (Delete, Remesh, Decimate, Retopologize, Mirror and weld, Slice, Make boolean, Extract, Delete higher and Delete lower, and Subdivide when the top level has sculpt layers), Smooth all and Project detail, each saying what it did and how long it took, with an Undo button at the right end: it is Ctrl+Z for that step, and it goes once the step is no longer the newest (undone, or another made since). Masking, hiding and clipping show by themselves and give no note. A long note is cut short on a narrow window; the Undo button always stays whole and on screen. The section that ran an operation shows the same line under its controls ("Last: ..."), and the Stats panel shows the last one in Last operation. A drag that starts on the viewport stays a stroke or orbit when it passes over a panel, and a press on a panel never reaches the viewport.

Panels and columns: the palette tray, Stats, History, the Objects card, the Colors card and the Thumbnail are panels, and so is any tray section pinned on its own (Pin as a panel, the button at the top of a section opened from a bar, or the Pin command in Search; every section but Objects pins). A panel is docked in a column, floating, or shown as a popover from its bar button. There are two columns: the right one beside the view rail under the top bar, where the tray docks at first, and the left one beside the off-hand island under the object chip. A column stacks its panels top to bottom and shares its height by what each wants: each gets all it asks for, or an equal share of what is left, and a panel taller than its share scrolls. Every panel's header has Panel options (the three dots; a right-click or long press on the header opens it too): Dock on the left or on the right, Float, Move up and Move down, Fold to its header (a folded docked panel shows a one-line summary: the brush, "Step 12 of 40", "3 objects", the colour, the light or layer count, "Level 2 of 4", "Dynamic on", the material, "Render on", the turntable's frames, the print scale, "Grid on"), Pin or Unpin, and Close. Drag a docked panel's header past the slop and it leaves its column and floats on under the finger; drag a floating panel over a column and the column lights up with a line where it will go, and letting go docks it there. While Settings, Help, Macros or Library is open, the right column hides, as the tray did. Columns, pins and their places are remembered (`column` and `pinned` lines in the settings file), and Settings > Interface > Reset panel positions (or Reset panels in Search) puts back the tray alone in the right column and unpins everything. Left-handed swaps the columns' sides, not their contents. **On a narrow window** (under 1180 points wide, as a tablet held upright; Settings > Interface > Columns pop over a narrow window, on at first, `column_popovers`) the columns pop over the view only while asked for, as the Lens report has columns become popovers from the rails, so a docked panel never covers a third of the view for good. A column put away leaves a tab at its top beside its bar (its icon, and how many panels it holds when more than one); a tap on the tab pops it, as does opening any panel docked in it (the tray's button, Stats, a pinned section, Show section, Search), and the tray's button then pops or puts away the column holding the tray rather than closing it. A press on the view puts a popped column away and goes no further, as a popover's dismissing press does, so it never sculpts; a press on a bar or another panel leaves it. Arranging or dragging a panel shows every column, for its outline and drop zone. Which column is popped is never remembered. Exit stats: `columns_narrow= column_popped= column_pops=`.

Spaces and workspaces (`src/app/spaces.odin`, `src/app/workspaces.odin`): the app works in three spaces, after Maxon Forger's contexts and Blender's workspaces. **Sculpt** is the brushes, Curve and Insert; **Model** is the Model tool (the cage); **Render** is the look's cards and the render view. The tool decides the space, so the two never disagree: picking a brush goes to Sculpt, picking the Model tool or adding a cage goes to Model, and Move stays in the space it was picked in. A tap on a space's tab (or Ctrl+1, 2 and 3; the command Space) gives back the tool last used there: Sculpt's brush or Curve, Insert or Move, Model's tool and action; Render keeps the tool in hand, since a press there turns the view. Selecting an object never switches the space. Each space has its own layout, the workspace current in it, and going back to a space brings its layout back as it was left. A layout is which bars show (the off-hand island, the view rail, the shelf, the modifier wheel and the action bar), which panels are open, the two columns, the pinned sections, where each floating panel sits, the viewport's pass (see Passes), the view grid and its panes' cameras and passes (see View grid), and the buttons of the view rail and the shelf's tools (see Customising the bars; the shelf's pages are the same in every layout, since they hold your brushes, and each space comes back on the page it was left on). **Each space has its own shelf pages**: Sculpt's Sculpt, Paint, Move and Curve with the tool strip after them; Model's Model page; and Render's Render page (`shelf_render`), with no tool strip: Render (at the Render section's quality), Full render, the Studio and Classic looks, Material, Lights and Environment (each opening its section beside the tile), Exposure as a chip to tap or scrub, Pick focus, Save screenshot, Record turntable and Render preview. The brush picker opened from there lists every brush, and Put on a bar puts a brush on Sculpt's first page; the scene, the brush and the view's toggles are no part of it. Three come with the app, one a space: Sculpt (Inspector open), Model (the Brush card, which holds the cage's palette under the Model tool, open; Layers, Scene and Symmetry folded) and Render (the stack holds only the Lights and Material cards, open, Environment, folded, and Palettes, and the mini view sits beside the stack's foot showing Render, the path trace building up with its count on the chip, "Render · 64/256", after round 2's studio2 screen). A layout that names the thumbnail's camera and pass (`thumbnail Follow Render`, as Render's does) writes them back; others leave them to the settings. A tap on the lit tab opens its menu: the space's layouts (its built-in and your own saved in it), then Save workspace (the dot goes), Save as a new workspace ("Workspace 1" and so on, up to eight of your own across the spaces, made current, and kept in the space it was saved in), Export workspace..., Import workspace..., Revert workspace (the saved layout back), and Reset workspace for a built-in (as it comes with the app) or Rename workspace... and Delete workspace... (which asks first, and gives the space its built-in) for your own; then the two views. **Focus mode** hides the view rail, the action bar and the off-hand island, leaving the model, the shelf, the dial and the top bar's three islands (below); **Inspect** shows the form four ways in the view grid (shaded, Curvature, Normals and a Front silhouette) with the off-hand island and the shelf aside, and its second tap puts the grid back as it was. Both are views, not layouts: they leave the layout's dot as it was, end on a space switch, and are in Search and the command palette too (`Focus_Mode`, `Inspect_Mode`). Export workspace... writes the current workspace, as it is now, to a small `.workspace` text file (its name, its space and its layout, never the scene, the brush or the shelf's pages), to carry a layout to another device or hand it to someone; Import workspace... reads one back as a new workspace of your own in the space it names (Sculpt for a file without one), named as it was saved (with " 2" and so on after it when that name is taken), and switches to it, the one left kept as it was. A file that is not a workspace, is damaged, or comes from a newer version of the app is refused with a note, and import is off while all eight of your own are in use. The file is text (`SCULPT_WORKSPACE 1`, then `name`, `space` and `layout` lines escaped as the settings file escapes them), so it reads and diffs in any editor; what a layout names that this version does not know is dropped on import. Switching keeps each workspace as it was left, changes and all, so going back loses nothing. The top bar never hides, and on a window too narrow for the tabs Search finds every space and workspace ("space", "workspace"). Settings > Interface > Reset panel positions also shows every bar again. Scripts switch with `space_switch model` (sculpt, model, render), `workspace render` (sculpt, model, render, own1 to own8), `focus_mode` and `inspect_mode` (`on` or `off`, or nothing to turn it over); an older script's `workspace focus`, `workspace inspect` and `workspace studio` turn Focus mode or Inspect on, or put Render's layout in place. The settings file (since version 5) keeps `workspace`, `workspace_space`, `workspace_saved`, `workspace_live`, `workspace_current` and `space_layouts` lines. A file from before spaces is read as it was meant: Studio's layout becomes Render's, your own workspaces keep theirs in Sculpt, a changed Focus or Inspect becomes a workspace of your own of that name, a current Focus or Inspect opens Sculpt with that view on, and a bar, key, gesture or pie that named the Focus, Inspect or Studio workspace runs Focus mode, Inspect or Render's layout. A file from before workspaces opens its layout as Sculpt's. Exit stats: `workspace= workspace_modified= workspace_count= workspace_menu= workspace_switches= workspace_exports= workspace_imports= zones_hidden=`, and `space= space_switches= focus_mode= inspect_mode=`.

Passes: the chip at the top bar's right end, before History ("Pass Shaded"), names how the viewport shows the meshes, and turns the accent colour while that is not Shaded. A tap opens the list: Shaded (as the materials and lights say), Normals (the surface's direction as a colour, as Blender's and ZBrush's normal views show it), Depth (near white, far black, across the scene, on black), Curvature (flat mid grey, creases toward blue and crests toward orange, whatever the light), AO only (a plain grey that occlusion while working darkens, on wherever the GPU can run it, at full strength) and Silhouette (every object near-black on paper, without the floor, as the thumbnail's silhouette but full size), with Last pass to go back. In Lens the list (`lens_pass_menu`, `src/app/passes.odin`) stands in three groups with each row's icon and keys: RECENT (the pass shown and the last other one, while they differ), SHADING (Shaded, the Sculpt studio and Classic looks, Flat shading and Render) and DIAGNOSTICS (Normals, Curvature, AO only, Depth, Silhouette), then Last pass and Wipe compare. Alt+1 to Alt+6 pick them, and Alt+0 (Last pass) goes back to the one before, so one key flips between two. Holding the chip (a finger, the pen or the mouse, past the long press) or the backquote key peeks at the last other pass, Normals when there is none yet, for as long as it is held. Only the viewport shows a pass: the thumbnail window, the navigation head, object pictures and a scene's preview picture stay shaded, while a screenshot, a time-lapse and a turntable show what the viewport does (no peek while those two record). The studio render and render mode pause while a pass shows and start over on Shaded. The pass is part of the workspace's layout (a `pass` line, only when not Shaded); a peek never is. Search finds them ("pass", "normals", "depth", "curvature", "ao", "silhouette"), and scripts pick one with `view_pass normals`. A window too narrow for the chip leaves the passes to Search and their keys. Exit stats: `pass= pass_shown= pass_menu= pass_switches=`.

Wipe compare (the pass list's last row, Search "wipe compare split", scripts `view_wipe`) splits the main view at a vertical line: the current pass left of it and the last other pass right of it (Normals when there is none), so Shaded against Normals, AO or Curvature reads at a glance, as Marmoset's and Substance's split views do. Both sides come from one draw, each pixel picking its pass by which side of the line it is on, and occlusion while working darkens only a side whose pass wants it. Each side's pass is named beside the line at the top. A drag on the round knob or anywhere along the line moves it, kept within the middle nine tenths of the view; that drag is the interface's, never a stroke or an orbit. Picking a pass while wiping changes the left side. The background, floor and Depth's range follow the left pass; the studio render and render mode pause while wiping, a peek is ignored, and the wipe is off while a time-lapse or turntable records. Only the main view wipes, even in a view grid. It is never saved, never part of a layout and never an undo step. Exit stats: `wipe= wipe_at= wipe_right= wipe_drags=`.

**Proportion guides** (the Camera menu's Proportion guides, Search "guides proportion", scripts `view_guides`; `src/app/guides.odin`) draw three level dashed amber lines, **Brow**, **Nose base** and **Chin**, across every level side view at once: the main view while it is orthographic and level (pitch and roll within half a degree, so the keypad's front, back and side views), each view pane and the thumbnail window looking from the Front, Back, Left or Right, and a Linked or Mirrored view of a level main view. Top, Bottom, perspective and tilted views show none. They are the portrait painter's face thirds and the height lines of a likeness setup, made live: the first time they are shown in a scene they sit at the thirds of the selected objects' bounds (else every shown object's, else a unit box), Chin at the bottom, Nose base a third up and Brow two thirds up. Each line's name sits in a tag at its right end, clear of a docked column; a drag on the tag moves that guide, and it moves in every view that shows it, so a feature's height reads the same from the front and the side. A line can be sculpted across, as only its tag takes the press. **Reset guides** (in the Camera menu while they show) puts them back at the thirds of what is selected now. They belong to the model, so the scene file keeps them (version 24: shown, seeded and the three heights; an older file opens with them off and seeds them when first shown), and Autosave notices a toggle or a finished drag; they never change the model, so they are never an undo step, as the camera is not. Exit stats: `guides= guide_heights= guide_lines=` (lines drawn in the last frame, over all views) `guide_drags=`.

Adjust last operation: right after Remesh (Ctrl+R or the Remesh section), Decimate, Mirror and weld, Retopologize, Extract masked, Smooth all or a Bend, Twist or Taper, the action bar shows the operation's name and Adjust..., which opens its settings above the bar: Remesh and Decimate the sizes (41k to 25M), Mirror and weld the axis and the side kept, Retopology the quad count, Extract the thickness, Smooth all the steps and strength, a deformer its Amount. A change runs the operation again from the state before it, not on its own result, so Decimate to 200k after Decimate to 500k is Decimate to 200k from the original, and a remesh is never a remesh of a remesh; a size or side runs at once, a slider when it is let go (at 10M a run takes seconds). The operation's undo step is replaced, so the History keeps one entry for it however many settings were tried, and the popover stays open with the result line to try another. A run that changes nothing (Decimate to more vertices than the object had) puts the earlier result back and says so. Anything after the operation ends the adjust: a stroke or mask, an undo, moving or selecting another object, changing its boolean mode, leaving HD or scrubbing the History. A Model mode drag (Extrude, Inset, Move, Extrude edge, Insert loop's slide, Bevel's width) shows its name and Adjust... too, whose Amount, dragged sideways, sets how far it went in place on its own undo step, following the drag. The command palette has it as Adjust last operation (greyed out when there is nothing to adjust), and opens it above the shelf when the action bar is hidden. Escape, Back or a press outside closes it.

Adjust last stroke (as ZBrush's Adjust Last): after a stroke (sculpting, masking or painting), Adjust last operation in the palette, or a key given to it, opens Amount above the shelf, and the Brush palette's Stroke section has the same slider as Last stroke under More options. It sets how much of the newest stroke stays, from 0% (as if it had not been drawn) through 100% (as drawn) to 150%, following the slider as it is dragged: every vertex the stroke moved, every mask or colour value it changed, and a recording layer's displacement go that share of the way from before the stroke to after it. It stays the stroke's one undo step, whose History entry then reads "Draw stroke (80%)"; undo takes the adjusted stroke back. There is no action bar group for it, which would show after every stroke. It applies while the stroke is the newest step on the level or HD window shown: another stroke, an undo, a level change or leaving HD ends it; not with dynamic topology, whose strokes change the triangles. The first adjust keeps a copy of what the stroke left of every part of the mesh it reached (12 bytes a vertex for positions or a layer's displacement, 1 for a mask, 4 for a colour, counted in the undo memory) until the step is undone or another comes. A replayed take or stroke file plays each stroke as it was drawn, at 100%.

The look comes from one set of design tokens (`src/ui/theme.odin`, one set per look: Lens, Studio, Graphite): the window's frame, surfaces, text, one accent and the status colours, the cameras' colours, three corner radii (6, 8, 12), five type sizes in Inter and the motion durations. **Lens**, the default, is the Lens design's look: a near-black frame round the views (the top bar, the gaps between view panes), graphite panels that step up in lightness the higher they float and cast a soft shadow on the view under them, opaque hairlines, sliders on a slate track, switches with a dark knob when on, the chosen tab outlined, the chosen brush on a faint wash of the accent with its icon in the accent, and one sea-green accent (Glaze, #5ED6C4) that no clay or skin tone is near, so a selection never reads as part of the model. The viewport's own background is the scene's and does not change with the look. The brush pie, the labels over the viewport and the Stats panel use the same tokens and typeface.

Motion (`src/ui/motion.odin`) follows Anime.js: a value eases toward its target over a duration, after an optional delay, and a new target sets off from wherever it is, so nothing jumps. Presses dip and wash, hover fades in, the marker under a chosen tab or brush slides, switches move their knob with a slight overshoot, menus and popovers grow from where they open and fade, groups open by growing, the tray slides, tooltips wait and fade, and notes rise in one after another. Nothing a stroke, the camera or a drag depends on is animated. Menu > Reduce motion (`reduce_motion on` in a script) makes every animation arrive at once; it is remembered.

The kit (`src/ui`) is drawn by the renderer's own UI pass (`src/render/ui_pass.odin`, `ui.vert.slang`/`ui.frag.slang`): one vertex and index buffer a frame, a single-channel glyph atlas updated only where it changed, one draw per texture and clip change, with the viewport image drawn as the first quad and the viewport's overlay (the brush outline, the gizmo, the shape being dragged) laid just over it, under the panels, though it is drawn after them, once the tools have run (`ui.layer_mark`). At 1600 x 900 a frame is about 6,500 vertices with the Brush palette open and 9,000 with the Object palette; laying it out, animating and tessellating it takes about 0.65 ms p50 (0.8 ms with the Object palette) in a release build on a 12-thread Windows desktop, at 1M and at 10M vertices alike (`ui_ms_p50`, `ui_ms_p99` and `ui_vertices` on exit). Animations change only the vertices drawn, never the layout, and the tweens live in one table allocated at start. Text is laid out once and kept: each line (its font, size and bytes) keeps the glyphs fontstash placed for it, kerning included, and later frames walk those glyphs from wherever the line is drawn, pixel for pixel what fontstash would place; wrapped paragraphs measure their lines from the whole paragraph's kept layout. A line unused for a second may go once 4,096 are kept (`src/ui/text_cache.odin`; `ui_text_lines`, `ui_text_layouts` and `ui_text_reads` on exit). That halved `ui_ms_p50` on the cloud machine (1.5 to 0.65 ms with the Lens stack open). Keys, the pointer and typed text come from SDL's events (`src/app/device.odin`); fingers and the pen come from SDL's touch and pen events (`src/app/touch.odin`, `src/app/navigate.odin`), with SDL's own turning of fingers into the mouse off (see Touch and pen below). Text is Inter (SIL Open Font License) and icons are Lucide (ISC), both embedded; their licences are in `assets/fonts`.

## Thumbnail

Camera > Thumbnail, the **second view** (the Camera menu, the command palette, or `thumbnail on` in a script), opens the **mini view** in the pen side's lower corner, under the panels, showing the whole scene in a view of its own, as a sculptor steps back from the work: ZBrush's thumbnail view, Blender's second 3D view and the painter's habit of flipping the picture to see its proportions afresh (`src/app/thumbnail.odin`, `src/app/lens_chrome.odin`, `src/render/thumbnail_view.odin`). A chip over the picture's corner names its camera and pass; a tap on it shows the choices under the picture, and a tap elsewhere on the picture swaps it with the viewport (the viewport takes the camera it sees through and it keeps the viewport's as Free, so a second tap swaps them back; a linked view has nothing to swap). **Camera** picks where it looks from: **Linked** (the default; `Follow` in files) sees through the viewport's camera; **Mirrored** sees through it reflected in the X symmetry plane, so it shows the other side from the matching place (the right cheek where the viewport shows the left), for symmetry and likeness checks; **Front**, **Back**, **Left**, **Right**, **Top** and **Bottom** look at the scene's middle from that side, orthographic, at the camera's distance, whatever the viewport shows, so a front view stays beside a three-quarter one for the likeness and the symmetry; **Free** is its own camera. A drag on the picture by finger, pen or mouse turns the camera it sees through: a linked or mirrored view turns the viewport's camera, so the viewport and every linked view turn together (a mirrored one with the sideways turn reversed, so its picture follows the finger); an independent one turns its own (it becomes Free, starting from what it showed; the viewport's camera stays still). The mouse wheel over it zooms the same camera. The **link** button beside Flip links an independent view (Linked) or lets a linked one go to Free at the view it shows, so nothing jumps. A **colour dot** before Camera says which camera it sees through: the main camera's colour (Lens: sea green) when linked, as a ring when mirrored, its own (violet) when independent. **Pass** picks what it shows: **Silhouette** (the default) draws every object black on a light ground, so the outline reads without the shading; **Shaded** draws it as the viewport does, lit by the scene's lights from its own camera; **Normals**, **Depth** and **Curvature** are the viewport's passes (Passes, above); then the heavy ones, which keep buffers of their own on the picture: **AO only** is the scene's occlusion alone, as the viewport's AO pass shows it (Scene > Look's Reach), and **Studio** is the studio render seen from this view, with its soft shadows and occlusion at the studio's strengths, gathering a sample a frame while the app rests until it has the studio's count (a frame that draws another picture waits; a stroke or turn starts it over once it is done); and **Render** is render mode's path traced picture seen from this view (Render mode, below: the scene's lights with soft shadows, the environment, materials, paint and maps, bounced light, Denoise, Half resolution and the lens from Scene > Render), a sample a frame at rest until Scene > Render's Samples, shown once it has a few and Shaded before that, starting over after a stroke or a turn. AO only and Studio need a device that reads depth in a shader, as live occlusion does; elsewhere they draw as Shaded. A view showing Render holds its own copy of the traced scene on the device, as render mode does, given back when it shows something else. **Flip** mirrors it left to right. It is drawn at twice its size and smoothed, so the silhouette's edge is clean. Each picture draws every object whole, without the viewport's culling or simplified copies, so it is drawn again only when the scene or its view changed, once a stroke or a turn of the view is over, and at most ten times a second (every frame while its own camera is dragged): while you work it costs nothing. Drag it by its dots; a long press on them or Close shuts it. Whether it is open, the camera (the Free camera's turn excepted), the pass and Flip are remembered (`thumbnail`, `second_camera`, `second_pass`, `thumbnail_flip`; an older file's `thumbnail_shaded 1` reads as Shaded). The exit stats print `thumbnail`, `thumbnail_draws`, `thumbnail_ms` (the CPU time of the last), `thumbnail_px`, `thumbnail_flip`, `second_camera`, `second_pass`, `second_turns` (frames a drag turned its own camera) and `second_linked` (frames a drag on it turned the viewport's). A release build's 120-frame stroke on a 2M sphere ran at the same frame time with it open as without (52 and 51 ms p50 on the VM's software GPU).

**View grid** (Camera > View grid, the command palette and Search: "grid", "split", "quad"; scripts `view_grid four`): the viewport splits into panes by a preset, or in Lens by the pane gestures (Pane gestures, above), after Blender's quad view and split areas and Maya's four views (`src/app/grid.odin`). **Single** (the default) is the viewport as it always was, filling the window; **Side by side**, **Stacked**, **One plus two** (a big pane on the left, two stacked on the right), **Four** and **Three across** split the whole window, the docked panels, bars and rails floating over the panes as they do over the view in Single (Settings > Interface > **Views run under panels**, on by default; off, the panes split the room between the docked columns, so no pane lies under one). Under a docked panel a pane's head keeps clear of it: right of a left column, and in two rows when a right column leaves too little room, so Link and Maximise stay in reach. The first pane (the left, the top or the big one, wherever a swap moves it) is the **main view**: the camera, the cursor, the pass chip, live occlusion, the studio and render mode work there exactly as in Single, and the tools too (and in any view pane while you work in it, below), and the pointer, the safe zone, the pies and the light markers follow it. The other panes are **view panes**, second views tiled beside it: each has its own **Camera** (Linked, Mirrored, Front, Back, Left, Right, Top, Bottom or Free, as in the second view) and **Shows** (Silhouette, Shaded, Normals, Depth, Curvature, or the heavy AO only, Studio and Render, as in the second view: a Studio or Render pane gathers its samples while the app rests, so a pane can show the studio render or the path traced render beside the view you sculpt in; working in it shows Shaded until the press is over) in its top left corner, after its colour dot, then the **link** button and **Maximise** to fill the grid with it and **Restore** to bring the grid back (never remembered). A new preset's panes start as the partners a sculptor wants: Front beside the view, Front and Right for three panes, Front, Right and Top for four, Shaded. **Render preview pane** (Camera > Render preview pane, after the View grid presets; the command palette and Search: "render preview"; scripts `render_preview`), after Blender's second 3D view in Rendered shading and the live render Marmoset and Substance keep beside the work: one tap puts the first view pane on Render, linked to the main camera (from Single the grid goes Side by side first; a grid with panes keeps its preset), so the path traced picture follows the view you sculpt in and builds up while you rest; again, the pane showing Render goes back to Shaded. Its row is checked while a view pane shows Render. **Work in any view** (Settings > Interface, on by default; `panes_work`): a press in a view pane works there exactly as in the main view, after Blender's quad views, each of which takes the tools: a brush stroke, a mask, a gizmo or tool drag, a one-finger turn on empty space, two fingers to pan and pinch, the mouse buttons by the mouse scheme, the pie, a double tap. It is seen through that pane's camera, so a stroke in the Front pane lands under the finger on the model's front. For as long as the press lasts the main view's role is lent to the pane: the pane draws live (the one view drawn live, as ever, so nothing costs more than sculpting in the main view), with its own pass, the brush cursor and the guides, and the main pane holds the picture it showed as the press began; the studio render, render mode, live occlusion and the wipe pause meanwhile and start over once it is given back. When the press is over the main pane draws live again and the view pane keeps what the gesture did to its camera: a turned, panned or zoomed pane becomes Free at the view it was left at (an axis view so turned is no longer that axis), while a linked pane's turn moves the main camera, and a mirrored one's moves it mirrored. A gesture that left the camera alone changes nothing about the pane, so a stroke never marks the workspace modified. A pen or mouse hovering a view pane shows no brush cursor until it presses. Off, a drag in a view pane only turns the camera it sees through, as in the second view: a linked pane turns the main view and every linked pane with it, an independent one only itself (it becomes Free). Either way the mouse wheel over a view pane zooms that pane. Every camera has a colour, after the Lens design: the main camera's is the first (Lens: sea green), and each independent pane's is its own (pane 1 violet, pane 2 amber, pane 3 pink); a pane's dot is the colour of the camera it sees through, so linked panes share the main colour and linking shows without reading, and with more than one pane the main pane is outlined in its colour. View panes draw every object whole, so like the second view they are drawn again only once a stroke or a turn of the view is over, at most ten times a second and one picture a frame, and every frame while their own camera is dragged: while you sculpt they cost nothing. Drag a divider between panes to move it (each pane keeps a tenth of the room and 120 points); while it is dragged the panes keep their pictures, stretched, and are drawn again at their new size when it is let go. A preset gives its usual splits, the panes already shown keeping their cameras and passes; the workspace's layout keeps the panes (a `panes` tree line, see Pane gestures, and `pane <1-3> <camera> <pass>` lines, only with more than one pane), and Inspect comes with Four: Follow with Curvature, Follow with Normals, and Front with Silhouette. A time-lapse or turntable recording keeps the grid it began with, and the benchmarks always run in Single. Screenshots, the scene's preview picture and the turntable show the main view. Exit stats: `grid= grid_panes= grid_split= grid_max= grid_drags= pane_draws= pane_ms= pane_px= pane_camera= pane_turns= pane_links= linked_turns= viewport_origin= grid_main= pane_work= pane_works= pane_work_dabs= pane_samples= pane_heavy= pane_render_samples=` (the view pane worked in now, presses worked in a view pane, the dabs they laid, the first view pane's studio samples, how many view panes show a heavy pass and the first view pane's render samples). A release 2M stroke in the main view with two Studio panes runs at the frame time it has with two Shaded ones (24.1 and 23.6 ms p50 on the VM's software GPU): the panes gather only at rest.

**View windows** (Search: "window", "monitor", "second screen"; the command palette's New view window; scripts `view_window`; and **Open in a window** on a view pane's head, after Maximise; desktop only, since Android gives an app one window): a second view in an OS window of its own, to put on another monitor, after Blender's extra main windows and the torn-off viewports of 3D-Coat and Marmoset (`src/app/view_window.odin`). It opens 960 by 640 points, titled Sculpt view 1 to 3 (three at most; a fourth says so), showing the pane's camera and pass it was opened from, or from the command the first view pane's in a grid with panes, else Linked and Shaded. Its picture fills it, with a head in its top left as a view pane has: the camera's colour dot (each window's independent camera has its own colour: Lens sky blue, lime and coral), **Camera**, **Shows**, the **link** button and **Close**. A drag on the picture by mouse, pen or its first finger turns the camera it sees through and the wheel zooms it, as on the second view: a linked window turns the main view and every linked view, an independent one only itself (it becomes Free). It is a view, not a second workspace: brushes stay in the main window, and keys typed while it has the focus are the main window's (Ctrl+Z, P, the brush keys), so it never swallows a shortcut. Its picture follows the view panes' rules (drawn whole, only at rest, one picture a frame shared with the panes and the second view; Studio and Render gather while the app rests) and is drawn at most 1920 by 1080 pixels' worth, shown larger smoothed, so a 4K monitor costs one 1080p drawing of the scene after a stroke; it shows no proportion guides. It is presented only when what it shows changed (a new picture or sample, its head, a resize or uncovering), with mailbox where the display offers it, so a window on a slower monitor never holds back the main window's frame; one that cannot be drawn into closes by itself and the app goes on. Its Close button or the OS close box closes that window only; closing the main window quits, closing them all. View windows are not remembered between runs (a monitor may be gone the next time). Close view windows (the command palette) closes them all. Exit stats: `view_windows= view_window_opens= view_window_draws= view_window_presents= view_window_refused= view_window_camera= view_window_pass=` (open now, opened in all, pictures drawn into them, frames presented, refused fourths, and the first window's camera and pass).
