A tap on another object selects it first; Invert (or Alt) changes nothing about which command a tap runs. A quick tap that stays within the slop latches a sector until it is tapped again (filled); a finger held on one makes it act only while held (lit), so the other hand sculpts with the pen or a finger and lifting ends it. Smooth and Mask latched or held together are Hide; otherwise one of them goes at a time, and Invert goes with any. A finger on a modifier sector is the wheel's alone: it is never the pointer, never part of a gesture, a hand resting there is no palm, and the pen landing leaves it be. A press takes the buttons' state as it goes down and keeps it until it lets go, so lifting the finger mid-stroke changes nothing; a held sector beats a latched one, the last held wins (but for Smooth and Mask held together, which are Hide), and any of Shift, Ctrl or Alt held at the press leaves the wheel out of it. The mouse and the pen click the sectors, which latches. Where a panel, a menu or a list lies over it, a finger there is the panel's. It sits under the brush bar (beside it where a short window brings the bar down to it) and rises above the bottom bar on a narrow one. Dragging the grip in its middle (a finger, the pen or the mouse) moves it anywhere in the window, and where it was put is remembered; Settings > Interface puts it at the bottom left or right (which also puts a moved wheel back), hides it (which lets go of every latch), and sets its size: Small, Medium (the default), Large, 2XL or 3XL; what its sectors show: Icons (the default), Icons and names, or Names (the reference app's sets name keyboard keys, but here Mask and Invert are both Ctrl, so the sectors name what they do instead); and its turn (Wheel turn, -180 to 180 degrees in steps of 15, so the hand finds each sector where it suits). Two fingers on the ring, spread or pinched, resize it to the nearest size, and twisted turn it: once the line between them grows or shrinks by twice the slop or turns 15 degrees, they stop holding their sectors (two held still still hide), latch nothing as they lift, and the wheel follows the whole spread and twist from where they came down. The second finger may land on the brush pie's sector or the grip; a lone finger there is still the pie's or the grip's.
- Settings > Touch turns each of these off: Lift one of two fingers to turn, Double tap to frame or pin.
- The gesture map (Settings > Touch, `src/app/gesture_map.odin`) says what eight gestures run, each Nothing or one of about 50 commands a hand without a keyboard reaches for (Undo, Redo, Brush pie, Pie 1 to 4, Search, the panels and the Objects card, framing, Previous and Next view, the view switches, Screenshot, Symmetry X, the mask's Invert, Clear, Blur, Sharpen, Grow and Shrink, Show all, Invert hidden, Hide masked and unmasked, grouping, polygroups, Solo, Show all objects, New folder, Subdivide, the levels, Remesh, Dynamic topology, Adjust last, the Move and Sculpt tools), or, through its last row, Any command..., whatever the Search lists: that opens the Search titled "Choose what ... runs", and the command picked there goes on the gesture instead of running. One-finger tap (Nothing): a quick tap of one finger on empty space (on the model a tap sculpts); with Double tap to frame or pin on it waits 0.3 s for a second tap first, and a pen coming near meanwhile drops it, as the hand's; with the Move tool it runs in place of clearing the selection. Two-finger tap (Undo), Three-finger tap (Redo), Four-finger tap (four or more fingers; Nothing), Hold two fingers (Undo), Hold three fingers (Redo), Hold four fingers (Nothing) and Pen side button (Brush pie). A hold fills its ring, then runs its command; Undo and Redo repeat while the fingers stay still, anything else runs once. The pen's side button, when not on the brush pie, runs its command where the pen is; the mouse's right click stays the brush pie. A gesture holds one command, so none collide; what overlaps is said under the map in the warning colour: four fingers the system may keep (Windows' touch gestures, some Android skins), the brush pie losing the pen's button, a hold that runs once, one command on two gestures, and a hold of your own sharing its start with Lift one of two fingers to turn. Every tooltip, the palette and Help > Gestures name the gestures the map gives a command ("Four-finger tap"), Help under YOUR GESTURES for a command it has no line for. Remembered as `gesture_map <slot> <command|none>` lines for the slots that differ; the older `tap_history 0` and `hold_history 0` lines still turn the two- and three-finger taps or holds off. The Brush pie command (Search, a gesture) opens the brush pie at the pointer for a tap.
- The pen's other ends and the Pencil's gestures (Settings > Pen, PENCIL AND BUTTONS, or the narrow panel's Touch tab under PEN; `src/app/pencil.odin`). An Apple Pencil's double-tap and the Pencil Pro's squeeze each run a command as a gesture-map slot does, as ZBrush for iPad lists them beside the finger taps: Pencil double-tap and Pencil squeeze start on Follow the Pencil setting, which does what the iPad's Settings > Apple Pencil asks, in sculpting's terms: the palette at the tip (squeeze's default there) opens the brush pie where the pencil hovers, or mid-screen when it is out of hover range; switching to the eraser (double-tap's default there) turns Smooth on or off, as the dial's Smooth sector does; switching to the last used brings back the brush held before; the colour palette opens the Colors card and the tool's attributes the brush's Stroke section. Ignore there turns the gesture off here too, whatever its slot holds, as Apple asks, and Run shortcut is the system's own. Any other command, or Nothing, takes the slot over, as Procreate and Concepts let a squeeze. A squeeze begun while a stroke or a drag is under way is the hand's grip and does nothing. Pen second button (Nothing): the barrel button nearer the top on pens with two (XP-Pen, Wacom), which SDL makes the middle mouse button; given a command it runs it and no longer drags as the middle button (a note says so). Eraser end (Smooth; or As the tip, Invert or Mask): the pen turned over strokes with that modifier, as holding the dial's sector does (Invert turns the brush or a held mode the other way), while a key held still decides. Remembered as `gesture_map Pen_Double_Tap|Pen_Squeeze|Pen_Button_2 <command|none>` and `pen_eraser`. On an iPad SDL 3.4 reports the pencil's lean wrongly (an upright pencil leans 90 degrees across), so the app works the lean out again from what SDL sends (`pencil_tilt_uikit`) and the Tilt brushes lean the same way on every platform. SDL 3.4 has no word of double-tap, squeeze or the Pencil Pro's haptics, so the iPad's own code in `ios/sculpt_ios.m` listens for them on SDL's view (UIPencilInteraction) and sends each gesture on with the iPad's preferred action; squeeze, Run shortcut, Ink attributes and the palette at the tip need iPadOS 17.5. Only Draw with Apple Pencil, in the iPad's Settings > Apple Pencil, is followed as Apple asks: while it is on a finger on the model turns the view whatever Settings > Touch says (the finger settings say so too), read again each time the app comes back to the front. A Pencil Pro gives a haptic where it is when something snaps into place under it: the view landing on another square view with Shift, each step of a snapped Move tool drag, a pie slice picked; and its other kind of haptic when a mask, hide or slice shape it drew is finished (UIKit's canvas feedback, which plays only for a Pencil Pro near the screen; the exit stats count them as `haptics`).
- Long press is the secondary action, as in ZBrush for iPad and Nomad (`secondary` in `src/ui/ui.odin`): a finger or the pen held on a control for the Hold delay (Settings > Touch, 0.25 s by default, down to 0.1 s, shorter than Android's 0.4 s and iOS's 0.5 s since a long press here is a tool used again and again) without moving past the slop does what a right-click on it does (the pen's side button is a right-click too), and a ring fills round the press from halfway (0.15 s at most) to show it coming. On a control with no such action it shows the control's name and shortcut instead. Either way letting go is no tap, and a press that moves, changes a value or scrolls is no long press. The mouse's left button never long-presses. What each does:
  - Undo and Redo open the History panel (while there is something to undo or redo; the History button beside them always does).
  - A brush or preset in the brush browser picks it and shows its settings in the tray.
  - A brush value on any bar opens its menu (its look, Brush settings, its moves), and a bar button with a small triangle in its corner opens its settings: Render and Floor grid their sections, Remesh its quick menu, Wireframe what it shows, Symmetry its X, Y and Z.
  - The Move, Curve and Insert tools on the shelf pick the tool and show its settings in the tray.
  - The chip bar's object chip opens the Scene palette (a tap opens the object's menu).
  - An object in the Scene palette opens its menu.
  - A done step on the History panel's bar makes it what the History brush paints back to (see History timeline and time-lapse); its note names the step, since the step's tooltip shows only on hover (the mouse, or a hovering pen).
- A finger or the pen changes nothing until it moves more than the slop, 10 points (Android's touch slop is 8 dp, iOS about 10 pt), so a tap never nudges the view; the mouse has none.
- The safe zone, as the SculptNavigation add-on's border: one finger, the pen or the left button pressed within 64 points of an edge of the screen (at the top, of the top bar's lower edge) turns the view even over the model, so when it is zoomed in until the model fills the screen there is still somewhere to turn it from. A faint line marks where the zone ends; panels over it keep their presses, and Ctrl keeps it for the mask and hide shapes (so does a finger held on Mask or Hide, though not a latched one). Settings > Camera sets its width (24 to 200 points), hides the line or turns it off.

Every touch, from its first finger down to its last finger up, belongs to where its first finger landed. On a panel the panels have it: that finger presses, drags and scrolls them, and other fingers of the same touch do nothing, so a hand resting on a panel never pinches the view. On the viewport the fingers navigate or sculpt as above, and lifting fingers never turns what is left into another gesture. A touch the system cancels lets go without a click, and a stroke it drew is taken back (Redo has it). Inside a panel that scrolls, a finger or pen press on a control waits to see which way it goes: up or down past the slop scrolls the panel and the control does nothing, sideways a slider takes it, and a tap within the slop is the control's tap; the mouse presses at once and scrolls with the wheel. Touchpads are not touch screens: their fingers never reach the view, and nor do the stand-in fingers SDL can make of the pen and the mouse.

`python scripts/gate.py` runs every check a change must pass (check, the Input diagnostics report analyzer's tests, test, build, smoke, these scenarios, the iPad compile check, and the Windows and Android compile checks on Linux) with one line a step. `python scripts/touch_check.py` (after a debug build) drives the app with scripted fingers, pen and mouse through these cases and checks the camera, the dabs, the undo steps and the saved settings on exit: a simulation on the desktop, not a tablet (see the `sm-touch-testing` skill).

## Input diagnostics

A developer's screen of guided input tests (`src/app/diagnostics.odin`, `diag_tests.odin`, `kit_diag.odin`), made for the iPad's input problems: a finger that stops working after the Pencil, the Pencil's hover moving sliders, sliders that feel different by finger and by Pencil, text fields and the keyboard, the bottom-edge Home swipe, and the barrel roll's direction. It records what the app's own input pipeline received and what its controls did, and exports a report that `scripts/input_report.py` analyses (here or on GitHub). It only watches: it reads each SDL event before the app's handlers do, reads the touch, pointer and interface state once each frame's interface is done, and its controls are the kit's own widgets; input is routed exactly as everywhere else.

**Where it is.** Search (the command palette) > Input diagnostics, or Settings > System > Statistics > Input diagnostics, in debug builds and in the developer iPad build (`.github/workflows/ipad.yml` builds the AltStore IPA with `SCULPT_DIAGNOSTICS=1`, which `build.sh ios` turns into `-define:SCULPT_DIAGNOSTICS=true`, and names the commit with `SCULPT_VERSION`). A build without the define, as a store build must be, does not list it anywhere (README's store rules: no debug panels reachable). `--input-diagnostics` opens it at the start in any build, `--diag-test=ID` opens it on that test and starts a run, and `--diag-export=PATH` writes the report on exit. It covers the window as Home does: nothing reaches the scene behind it, its keys are its own, and finger and Pencil gestures (two-finger taps, double-tap, squeeze) run nothing while it shows; the log still records them. Escape or the close button closes it; the session stays until the app ends, to go on with or to export.

**The screen.** The tests down the left (a list at the top on a window under 1000 points wide), the test shown in the middle, and on the right the controls the tests use: the pad (an area to tap, drag and hover over, which draws the trail of where the pointer went: filled dots while touching, rings while hovering), the size slider (a tall slider like the off-hand island's, outside any scrolling), and in a scrolling column like the panels' (so a finger's press on them waits to see if it scrolls, as in the tray) the slider, the diagnostic button, the diagnostic menu and the diagnostic field, then the live values: the last event, where, the pointer and whether it touches, pressure, hover height, tilt, the roll as reported and as applied this frame, whether the app thinks the pen is near or down, fingers and palm, who owns the touch, the control under the pointer, the values, whether the field has the keys, text input and the keyboard, and the run's counts. A test's control is ringed. Each test shows its device (finger, Pencil, both, or keyboard), what it needs, its steps and what should happen. Start begins a run (Repeat a new one), Skip ends it as skipped and moves on, Next ends it and moves on. While it runs the card shows the automatic verdict so far, judged from the run's own counts where one is objective (pass, fail, inconclusive, or unavailable when the device reports nothing to judge, such as no pressure: never a 0), what it noticed, your verdict (Pass, Fail, Inconclusive, Skip) and a notes field for what you saw. Keep recording after closing logs on while you use the app's own panels, so a problem with the real island sliders or Search goes in the report too.

**Received and answered.** Every verdict keeps what arrived apart from what a control did: the raw downs, moves and ups by finger, Pencil and mouse (and which landed on the test's control), against the control's presses, releases, clicks, value changes, focus and text. A finger down on the pad that the pad never answered is "received, not answered", and the log says whether the app had taken it for a palm and whether it still thought the Pencil near.

| Test | Device | Needs | Checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `finger_tap` | finger | iPad | three taps on the pad are three presses and releases; nothing stays pressed |
| `finger_drag` | finger | iPad | a drag across the pad is followed to its lift |
| `finger_cancel` | finger | iPad | a touch the system cancels (Control Center) lets go without a click (inconclusive with no cancel) |
| `finger_after_pencil` | finger, Pencil | iPad, Pencil | finger taps within two seconds of putting the Pencil down are all answered, not palms |
| `finger_pencil_switch` | finger, Pencil | iPad, Pencil | five changes between Pencil and finger, every tap answered |
| `pencil_tap` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | three Pencil taps, three presses |
| `pencil_drag` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | a Pencil line across the pad is followed |
| `pencil_hover` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | hovering shows no press, and the Pencil taken away is known to have left within two seconds |
| `pencil_hover_contact_lift` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | near, down, up, then leaving, in that order |
| `pressure_ramp` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | pressure from light to hard covers at least half its range; unavailable without pressure reports |
| `hover_slider` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | the Pencil hovering on the slider, unturned, never changes it |
| `hover_button` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | hovering the button never clicks it |
| `hover_menu` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | hovering the open menu's rows never picks one |
| `slider_ratio_finger` | finger | iPad | the size slider moves exactly as far as the finger along its track (within 15%) |
| `slider_ratio_pencil` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | the same by Pencil: the report compares the two |
| `text_focus_finger` | finger | iPad | a tap gives the field the keys, text input comes on, the keyboard shows |
| `text_focus_pencil` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | the same by Pencil |
| `text_typing` | keyboard | iPad | sculpt typed and two Backspaces leave scul |
| `text_dismiss_reopen` | keyboard | iPad | the keyboard's hide key lets the field go; a second tap brings it back |
| `text_scribble` | Pencil | iPad, Pencil | handwriting in the field becomes text (Scribble) |
| `home_swipe_baseline` | finger | iPad | one swipe up from the bottom edge goes Home, before the Pencil is used: your verdict, with when the app went to the background |
| `home_swipe_after_pencil` | finger, Pencil | iPad, Pencil | the same after drawing with the Pencil |
| `roll_right` | Pencil | iPad, Apple Pencil Pro | the Pencil Pro hovering on the slider and rolled right (its top side to the right, clockwise seen from its end) raises the value |
| `roll_left` | Pencil | iPad, Apple Pencil Pro | rolled left lowers it |
| `roll_neutral` | Pencil | iPad, Apple Pencil Pro | held still, the value stays within 0.5 of 100 |
| `roll_wrap` | Pencil | iPad, Apple Pencil Pro | rolled a full turn, the value moves on past the angle's wrap from 180 to -180 with no jump |

Every test is meant for the iPad: on a desktop a mouse stands in for none of the finger or Pencil tests (their verdict says inconclusive and why), and the home swipe, Scribble, pressure and roll need the device itself. The scripted scenarios below drive the same screen with SDL's own finger and pen events on the desktop.

**What it notices** as it happens (anomalies, each logged once a run and counted after): `received_not_responded`, `finger_palm` (a finger taken for the hand holding the Pencil, with whether the Pencil was near and how long since it last reported), `pen_near_stuck` (the app still thinks the Pencil near two seconds after its last report: SDL's iPad driver never says the Pencil left), `hover_changed_value`, `roll_jump`, `roll_wrong_direction`, `stuck_pressed` (something held 0.3 s with nothing touching), `down_without_up`, `up_without_down`, `os_cancel`, `text_without_focus`, `keyboard_desync` (a field with the keys and text input off, or the keyboard up with no field), `background_during_step`, `pressure_unavailable`, `frame_gap` (a frame over 0.25 s while touching, rests left out) and `log_truncated`.

**The report.** Export report (top right) saves one ZIP holding `diagnostic-report.md` (the session, every test's runs with both verdicts, reasons and notes, and the anomalies), `diagnostic-events.jsonl` (the log, one JSON object a line) and `diagnostic-summary.json` (the session, raw counts, every test with its runs and metrics, and the first 200 anomalies). On a desktop it is the save dialog; on the iPad the app asks for a name, then for the folder (Files' On My iPad > Sculpt, iCloud Drive, ...), from where Files shares it by AirDrop or Mail. The log's first line says what made it: the session id, app version and build, platform, the iPadOS version and model (`iPadOS 26.7.2`, `iPad14,8`), the window, and the Home indicator hint. Every other line has the time from the session's start, a sequence number, its kind, and the test and run it belongs to: `event` (a raw SDL event: source finger, pen, mouse, key, text, window or app; type; pointer id; position in points and movement; pressure; a pen axis and its value, with `wrap` where the roll's angle wrapped), `state` (the pipeline when it changed: pointer kind, held, cancel, touch owner, fingers, palm, Pencil near and down, the held control, the field with the keys, text input, keyboard, pie; with the hover height and roll), `control` (what a test control did, by which pointer, touching or not, with values), `step`, `anomaly` and `result` (both verdicts, the reason, your notes and the metrics). It is bounded: a moving pointer is logged at most every 50 ms or 24 points, the moves between folded into a count, pen axes when they change by a step; never a line a frame. The log holds at most 4 MB (moves and axes stop at 3 MB); past that only steps, results and anomalies go in, with a line saying it was cut. Typed text is logged only from the diagnostic field (whose text is the test's own, sculpt or ok); keys that type are logged as `printable`, never by name, and your notes are the only other words in it.

**Analysing a report.** `python scripts/input_report.py REPORT [--out DIR] [--strict]` reads a report ZIP, its folder or its `diagnostic-events.jsonl` (`--url URL` fetches one first) and writes `input-analysis.md` and `input-analysis.json` to DIR (`build/input-report` by default). It checks the log against its schema and ties every event to its test and run, then sorts what it finds into three lists that never mix: confirmed failures (a run's own automatic fail backed by its events, or your Fail), suspicious patterns (missing events such as a down without an up or a Pencil that never left, inconsistent transitions, odd timing, and the runs before and after the Pencil compared: finger taps, the slider ratio by finger and by Pencil, the Home swipe, field focus), and what needs your interpretation (inconclusive or unavailable verdicts, tests only you can judge, your verdict and the app's disagreeing). It says what each finding correlates with and never claims a cause: a log shows what happened, and the iPad confirms why. `python scripts/test_input_report.py` runs it on the samples in `scripts/input_report_samples/` (a healthy session, one showing the reported iPad bugs, and a damaged one); `gate.py` runs that as its `reports` step.

**On GitHub.** The Input tests workflow (`.github/workflows/input-tests.yml`) runs `gate.py --skip smoke` on Linux when started by hand (Actions > Input tests > Run workflow, or `gh workflow run input-tests.yml --ref BRANCH`), never by itself, since a run takes about half an hour of the private repository's monthly Actions minutes: check, the analyzer's tests, every package's tests, the debug app, all of touch_check's scenarios on virtual displays with Mesa's software Vulkan (the finger, pen, palm, roll and Input diagnostics scenarios among them), and the iPad, Windows and Android compile checks; `build/gate/` is kept as its artifact. Smoke stays a local step: it asks for GPU validation layers and checks drawing, not input. The Input report workflow (`.github/workflows/input-report.yml`, started by hand) analyses one report: either commit it on a branch under `diagnostics/reports/` (git ignores that folder, so add it with `git add -f diagnostics/reports/ipad-2026-10-29.zip`, push the branch, and give that path and branch when starting the workflow), or give an https link to the ZIP. It runs the analyzer's tests, then the analysis (with Strict on, confirmed failures fail the run), shows `input-analysis.md` on the run's page and keeps both files as the `input-analysis` artifact. No workflow can reach the iPad. The iOS simulator is not used: SDL's iOS build and the simulator need a Mac with Xcode, which only the macOS runner has, and scripted events there would test the same routing the desktop scenarios already do, not UIKit's touch and Pencil delivery, palm rejection or system gestures, which only the device has.

**Running it locally.** `build\sculpt.exe --diag-test=finger_tap` (or `./build/sculpt`) opens a test; `python scripts/touch_check.py 'diag_*'` runs its desktop scenarios (finger taps answered, a Pencil that left and one that never says so, hover, roll right, left and past the wrap, the size slider's ratio, typing, Escape, the export), and `--diag-export=PATH` with any of them writes the report to look at or to feed to `scripts/input_report.py`. The exit stats give `diag_open diag_runs diag_lines diag_bytes diag_dropped diag_exports`, and for the last run `diag_test diag_auto diag_palms diag_presses diag_finger_downs diag_pen_downs diag_anomalies`, with `diag_slider diag_vslider diag_field`.
